# The Bhrigu Samhita: Section on Horoscopes
<note>The Bhrigu Samhita is a legendary collection of astrological predictions attributed to the Vedic sage Bhrigu.</note>

## That is, The Sun of Universal Birth-Charts

### With vernacular commentary and examples.

In this book, you will find the nine planets <note>The Navagraha: Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, Saturn, and the lunar nodes Rahu and Ketu</note> for anyone born in the last 81 years, and their fortunes will be accurately revealed. You can reconstruct a lost birth chart or rectify an existing one. It contains many useful tables, a list of eclipses up to the year 2000, and a 4300-year calendar. Many newspapers have reviewed this work and **certificates** <note>original: "साटीफिकेट" (transliterated English 'certificate')</note> of merit have been received. By the influence of this book, astrologers have become perfected masters. Some even claim it possesses the power of a "Karna Pishachini" <note>A tantric spirit said to whisper secrets or the past/future into a practitioner's ear</note>, and so on. For a four-month promotional period, the discounted price is 2 Rupees (postage 8 Annas); after this, it will not be available even for 100 Rupees. Only a few books remain.

->Address—Jyotiratna (Jewel of Astrology), Vaidyabhushan (Ornament of Physicians) Pandit Ayodhya Prasad Mishra, Astrologer,<-
->Jhansi, U.P. JHANSI.<-

---

Pandit Ramlal Sharma, Head of the Astrology Office (Jalesar)
Date: 10-3-1917 <margin>District (Etah)</margin>

### Poetic Verse (Kavitta)

> In every way, the Sahaswan <note>Likely referring to a specific lineage or place of power</note> power is favorable; this pure text demonstrates the profound influence of mathematical calculation.
> Among the assemblies of learned men from all lands and nations, it has received great honor and the highest respect.
> Born of the lineage of Gangadhar Mishra, the famous astrologer and physician named Ayodhya Prasad has created this form.
> For the improvement of all and for the benefit of the public, he has authored "The Sun of Universal Birth-Charts." ||1||
>
> **Poetic Verse (Kavitta)** || Observe the vast world through the Bhrigu Samhita; match the nine planets of birth with the horoscope.
> Verify the Era <note>Samvat: The Vikram Samvat calendar era</note>, the month, the date, the day, the ascendant, and the degree of the Sun, and let the holy results blossom in your heart.
> "The Sun of Universal Birth-Charts" is a famous and useful text; drink the nectar that is its essential substance.
> It is an ocean contained within a jar <note>A metaphor for a concise but vast work</note>, bringing light to astrology; through charity and duty, give life to the humble folk. ||2||

# The Sun of the World's Horoscopes Almanac
<note>original: "Janmank Bhasker Panchanga" - 'Janmank' refers to a birth chart, 'Bhasker' means the Sun, and 'Panchanga' is a traditional Hindu calendar/almanac.</note>

->—By—<-

### PANDIT AYODHYA PRASAD MISRA, ORNAMENT OF PHYSICIANS AND ASTROLOGER.
<note>original: "Vaidya Bhushan" - A title meaning 'Jewel' or 'Ornament' of Ayurvedic medicine.</note>

->The recipient of thanks and honor for a Horoscope prepared for His Imperial Majesty the KING EMPEROR. The proprietor of the Ayurvedic Medical Hall, Jhansi. The recipient of a 1st Class Certificate of honor and a Silver medal (diploma) from the President and Judges of the All India Ayurvedic Conference, 5th Session, Mathura, and the Grand Ayurvedic Exhibition held at Mathura in 1914 A.D.<-

->*Also a member of the Divisional Malaria Committee, Jhansi.*<-

# (The Sun of the World's Horoscopes Almanac)
## || Vernacular Commentary with Examples ||
### || Composed by the Astrologer Pandit Ayodhya Prasad Misra, Ornament of Physicians ||

->Honored by the Government and recipient of a Silver Medal and First Class Certificate from the Great Circle of Ayurveda <note>The All India Ayurvedic Congress</note>. As a member of the Divisional Malaria Committee, the author has written this book using examples of constants from the year 1203 <note>likely a reference to a specific astronomical epoch</note> or based on the four planets. It fully describes how to reconstruct a lost birth chart based on the Era, the Shaka year, month, lunar fortnight, date, day of the week, lunar mansion, planetary yogas, and the ascendant. It also includes the fruits of the nine planets, royal yogas <note>auspicious planetary alignments for success</note>, yogas for death and longevity, and predictions for the sex of an unborn child. It contains a truly useful table for the section on auspicious timings, such as the compatibility chart for brides and grooms, eclipses for the next 27 years, a 4,300-year calendar (yearly tables), tables for the ascendant and marriage timings, and printed almanacs from the years 1600 to 1950.<-

<meta>This page serves as the formal introduction (Bhumika) to a new astrological almanac. It establishes the lineage of the author and the technical basis of the calculations.</meta>

# Introduction

---

In this world, having taken birth, it is the duty of noble men to earn wealth through morality and justice and to dedicate their intellect to the welfare of all living beings. Keeping this in mind, in this almanac titled **The Sun of the Birth-Chart** <note>original: "Janmāṅga Bhāskara"</note>, a new table of ascendants <note>term: Lagna Sāriṇī; the table used to calculate the rising sign at birth</note> has been constructed based on a precession of twenty-three degrees <note>term: Ayanāṃśa; the longitudinal difference between the tropical and sidereal zodiacs</note>. Currently, in the land of India—the ornament of the earth—all twice-born <note>term: Dvija; referring to the three upper social classes in traditional Indian society</note> and other people follow the path of religious propagation established by the great sages, who were experts in all Vedas and scriptures and excelled in penance. By determining the auspicious and inauspicious fruits of birth and performing good deeds accordingly, they attain their desired happiness. For those virtuous people who have entered the path of religion established by those various sages, the realization of auspicious or inauspicious results arises only through the performance of those specific rituals. Regarding this subject, if astrologers or other scholars, upon careful examination of this **Sun of the Birth-Chart** almanac, find any errors—whether small or great—arising from natural human oversight or confusion, I beg forgiveness for such offenses. I request that they kindly correct them and grant permission to print those corrections in the next edition. I offer this prayer to the wise. May there be peace.

> || Verse || In the famous city of Jhansi, within the great king's domain on this earth, there is a place known as the auspicious Sagar Gate in the east, where the fulfiller of desires always resides. || 1 ||

> || Verse || There lived the famous and virtuous Gangadhara Mishra, the jewel of his lineage. In his family was the scholar of the Puranas, the one named Rammi Lala, who was devoted to the gods and teachers. || 2 ||

> || Verse || He had two sons, excellent like the sons of gods, who were the best among the knowers of the Absolute. The first was named Ghanashyam, and the second was Ayodhya Prasad, the greatest among physicians. || 3 ||

> || Verse || This almanac, which acts like the sun to the lotus of the birth-chart, is created for the joy of the knowers of the Vedas. It is surely intended for the knowledge of the younger brothers of astrologers <note>a humble way of referring to students or fellow practitioners</note> by those expert in the science of calculation. || 4 ||

-> Seeker of Grace <-
-> Honored by the Government <-
### Astrologer Pandit Ayodhya Prasad Vaidyabhushan
-> (Silver Medalist) Member of the Divisional Malaria <-
-> Committee, Ayurvedic Practitioner <-
-> JHANSI. <-

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page serves as the introductory frontispiece and opening instructional section of a mathematical astrology manual. It establishes the author's intent and provides the first formula for calculating birth details.</meta>

# || Sri Hari: Om ||
## Salutations to the Great Lord Ganesha

-> || Verse || I bow again and again to the lotus feet of Lord Rama, <-
-> who destroys the suffering of his servants and grants happiness in heaven and on earth. ||1|| <-
-> Having bowed to the elephant-headed Ganesha, and likewise to the Guru and Goddess Sharada <note>Saraswati, the goddess of learning</note>, <-
-> I create this birth-chart almanac according to my understanding. ||2|| <-

-> || Verse || This "Sun of Birth-Charts" <note>original: "Janmanka Bhaskara"</note> almanac is for the delight of astrologers. <-
-> It was composed by me in the city of Jhansi with great labor for the sake of the righteous. <-
-> Desiring the welfare of my kinsmen, so that there is no omission of sacred fasts, <-
-> may my countrymen and brothers accept this with great joy. ||1|| <-

**Meaning ||** For the pleasure of astrologers <term>Jyotirvid [one who knows the lights/stars]</term> and for the welfare of my religious brothers, I have created this "Janmanka Bhaskara" almanac in the city of Jhansi with great effort. This has been composed according to the wishes of my brothers so that there is no lapse in observing fasts and rituals. May the noble gentlemen gladly accept this work intended for the propagation of astrology. ||1||

### ( 1 ) Knowledge of the Birth Year and Month

-> || Verse || Multiply the month-number by two, add five (5), <-
-> and multiply the result by fifty (50). To the resulting number, add the years passed, <-
-> subtract three hundred sixty-five (365), and add one hundred fifteen (115). <-
-> By the grace of Lord Shiva, the resulting number will reveal the time of birth. <-
-> This method is quick to perform and is approved by the learned. ||2|| <-

**Meaning ||** Multiply the month-number by 2 and add 5. Then multiply by 50. To the result, add the number of years passed <note>the person's current age</note>. Subtract 365 and then add 115. This will reveal the birth month and the years passed. ||2||

**Example ||** For instance, if a birth occurred in the month of Phalguna <note>the 12th month of the Hindu lunar calendar, roughly February/March</note> in the Era <term>Samvat [the Vikrama Samvat calendar era, starting 57 BCE]</term> 1962, and we wish to calculate it in the year 1965: The month-number for Phalguna is 12. Multiplying this by 2 gives 24. Adding 5 gives 29. Multiplying this by 50 gives 1450. Adding the 3 years of age passed gives 1453. Subtracting 365 from this leaves 1088. Now, adding 115 results in 1203. This is the final fixed number. By providing this number, the birth horoscope can be prepared. From the time of birth and the name of the zodiac sign, the results for the person's entire life can be obtained. 12

...from the month of Phalguna, and by subtracting from the Year 1665 of the <term>Samvat</term><note>The Vikrama Samvat is a historical Hindu calendar era beginning in 57 BCE.</note> era, the birth is said to be in 1362.

## Knowledge of Shaka and Common Era years from the Samvat year

### Verse
> Subtracting 135 from the year of the Vikrama era
> results in the Shaka year; adding 78 to that
> creates the year of the Common Era. || 3 ||
<note>original: "हीनाः शर त्रिशाशि १३५ भिर्वैकमीयस्तु वत्सरः । शकाब्दं सोपि कुरुते बसु सप्त ७८ युतः सनम् ॥ ३ ॥"</note>

### Meaning
By subtracting 135 from the Samvat year, the <term>Shaka Shalivahana</term><note>A historical calendar era beginning in 78 CE, widely used in Indian astronomy.</note> year is found. Similarly, by adding 78 to the Shaka year, the Common Era (A.D.) year is determined. || 3 ||

### Example
By subtracting 135 from Samvat 1362, the remainder is Shaka 1227. By adding 78 to that Shaka year, the year is 1505. The year 1505 lasted until Monday, the 5th day of the bright half of the month of Pausha; since this birth occurred in Phalguna <note>Phalguna follows Pausha in the lunar calendar</note>, the year became 1506. || 3 ||

---

## Determining the Fasli year from the Shaka year

### Verse
> Now, from the Shaka year, take five hundred and fifteen 515;
> the remaining figure shall be the year of the Yavanas. || 4 ||
<note>original: "शकाब्दाङ्क मपी दानीं शरेन्दु विशिखैः ५१५ कुरु ॥ रहितं सभवे दङ्को यावनी यस्तु वत्सरः ॥ ४ ॥"</note>

### Meaning
By subtracting 515 from that same Shaka year, the <term>Fasli</term><note>A harvest-based calendar introduced during the Mughal era; "Yavana" here refers to its Perso-Arabic origins.</note> year is found. || 4 ||

---

## (Knowledge of the Sun's Northern and Southern Course)

### Verse
> From Capricorn to the end of Gemini, the Sun moves in the North;
> Likewise, from Cancer to the end of Sagittarius, it is always fixed in the South. || 5 ||
<note>original: "मकराज्मिथुनान्तन्तु सूर्योयात्युत्तरे गहि । यथा कर्काद्धनान्तन्तु दक्षिणेन सदाध्रुवम् ॥ ५ ॥"</note>

### Meaning
Know that from the Sun being in Capricorn until it reaches the end of Gemini, it is <term>Uttarayana</term><note>The northern progress of the sun, considered an auspicious half of the year.</note>. From Cancer until the Sun reaches the end of Sagittarius, it is <term>Dakshinayana</term><note>The southern progress of the sun.</note>. || 5 ||

### Example
In the birth chart, the Sun is in Aquarius; therefore, the birth is said to be during the Northern Course (Uttarayana).

---

## Knowledge of birth in the Bright or Dark Fortnight

### Verse
> If the Moon is within seven places from the Sun, the wise say birth is in the Bright half;
> Beyond that, it is the Dark half, determined by the positions of the Sun and Moon. || 6 ||
<note>original: "सूर्यात्सप्तन्तरे चन्द्रे शुक्ले जन्म वदे बुधः । ततोर्ध्वं कृष्ण पक्षेचरवि चन्द्र विचारतः ॥ ६ ॥"</note>

### Meaning
If the Moon is located within seven houses from the Sun, the birth is in the <term>Shukla Paksha</term><note>The waxing phase of the moon.</note>. Beyond that, it should be known as the <term>Krishna Paksha</term><note>The waning phase of the moon.</note>. || 6 ||

### Example
As seen in the birth chart of Balkrishna, the Moon is within 7 signs of the Sun; therefore, the birth is in the Bright Fortnight (Shukla Paksha). || 6 ||

->The Sun-like Almanac of Birth Charts<-

## || Knowledge of the Lunar Day ||

### || Verse || The New Moon is said to occur where the Sun resides in the birth chart. ||
### Multiply the count from the Sun to the Moon by 2.5 (2½) to find the Lunar Day. || 7 ||

**Meaning:** In a birth chart <note>Janma Kundali</note>, consider the house where the Sun is located as the New Moon <note>Amavasya</note>. Count the houses from the Sun to the Moon and multiply that number by 2.5 to determine the specific Lunar Day <note>Tithi</note> of birth. || 7 ||

**Example:** In the birth chart of Balkrishna, the Moon is in the third house from the Sun. Thus, three multiplied by 2.5 equals 7.5. Since the Sun is at 16 degrees, we take an additional half-day, concluding the birth occurred on the eighth day <note>Ashtami</note> of the lunar cycle. || 7 ||

---

## || Method for Determining Day or Night Birth ||

### || Verse || If the Ascendant is within seven houses from the house occupied by the Sun, ||
### the wise should declare a day birth; otherwise, it is a night birth. || 8 ||

**Meaning:** If the Ascendant <note>Lagna; the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> is within seven houses from the Sun, it is a day birth. If it is between the seventh and twelfth houses, it is a night birth. Furthermore, if the Ascendant is exactly seventh from the Sun, it is evening; if the Sun is in the Ascendant, it is morning. Alternatively, if the Sun is in the 2nd or 3rd house from the Ascendant, birth is in the latter half of the night; if in the 4th, near midnight; if in the 5th or 6th, in the first half of the night; if in the 7th, near sunset; if in the 8th or 9th, near midday; and if in the 11th or 12th, before midday. || 8 ||

**Example:** In Balkrishna’s birth chart, the Ascendant is ninth from the Sun. Since this is more than a seven-house interval, it is a night birth. Alternatively, the Sun is fifth from the Ascendant, so the birth is said to be before midnight. || 8 ||

---

## Knowledge of the Weekday at the Time of Birth

### || Verse || Multiply the months starting from the bright half of Chaitra by 1.5 (1½). ||
### Add the elapsed lunar days and divide the total by seven (7). || 9 ||
### The wise should know the remaining number as the day, counting from the Lord of the Year. ||
### The day of the first of the bright half of Chaitra is the Lord of the Year. || 10 ||

**Meaning:** Count the months starting from the first day of the bright half of the month of Chaitra <note>the Hindu New Year</note> and multiply by 1.5. Add the number of elapsed lunar days to this, then divide by seven. The remainder indicates the day of the week, counting from the "King" <note>the planet ruling the first day of the year</note>. The weekday on which the first of Chaitra falls is the King of that year. The Kings for the years 1600 to 1680 <note>likely Vikram Samvat era, approx. 1543–1623 CE</note> are listed in the Almanac section. || 9 || 10 ||

<note>Technical terms used: Tithi (Lunar day), Janma Kundali (Birth chart), Surya (Sun), Chandrama (Moon), Lagna (Ascendant), Chaitra Shukla Pratipada (1st day of the bright half of Chaitra), Vatsaradhipati (Lord of the Year), Panchang (Almanac).</note>

**Example:** From the 1st of the bright half of <term>Chaitra</term><note>The first month of the Hindu calendar, roughly March-April</note> to the 30th of the dark half of <term>Phalguna</term>, there are 12 months. Multiplying this by 1.5 gives 18.5. Adding the 8 days of the elapsed <term>Paksha</term><note>A lunar fortnight (15 days)</note> results in 26.5. Dividing this by 7 leaves a remainder of 3.5. Counting from the "King" of the year 1962 <insert>Samyat era</insert>, which is Mercury, the birth is declared to be on a Saturday. ॥9॥10॥

### Knowledge of the Nakshatras <note>Lunar Mansions</note>

**Verse:** Count the months starting from <term>Kartika</term>, double them, and add the elapsed lunar days (<term>Tithis</term>). Divide by twenty-seven; to the remainder, add one. This will certainly be the <term>Nakshatra</term>. ॥11॥

**Meaning:** Count the months starting from Kartika and double that number. Add the elapsed lunar days to it and divide the total by 27. Add one to the remainder. The resulting number, when counted starting from <term>Ashwini</term><note>The first of the 27 lunar mansions</note>, indicates the birth Nakshatra. ॥12॥

**Example:** From Kartika to <term>Magha</term> there are 4 months. Doubling this gives 8. Adding the 23 elapsed lunar days results in 31. Dividing by 27 leaves a remainder of 4. Adding one more makes 5. Thus, the birth is said to be in <term>Mrigashira</term><note>The 5th Nakshatra, symbolized by a deer's head</note>.

---

### Knowledge of the Yogas <note>Astrological joinings/alignments</note>

**Verse:** Count from <term>Pushya</term> to the Sun's Nakshatra, and from <term>Shravana</term> to the birth Nakshatra. Combine them and divide by the number of Yogas <insert>27</insert> to state the Yogas in order. ॥12॥

**Meaning:** Count from the Pushya Nakshatra to the Nakshatra where the Sun is positioned. Then count from Shravana to the birth Nakshatra. Add these two figures together and divide by 27. The remainder, when counted from <term>Vishkumbha</term><note>The first of the 27 Yogas</note>, reveals the specific Yoga. ॥12॥

**Example:** From Pushya to the Sun's Nakshatra (<term>Purva Bhadrapada</term>) is 18. From Shravana to the birth Nakshatra (Mrigashira) is 11. Adding them together gives 29. Dividing by 27 leaves a remainder of 2. Thus, the birth is said to be in the <term>Priti</term> Yoga. ॥12॥

---

### Knowledge of the Fixed Karanas <note>Half-lunar days</note>

**Verse:** In the first half of the first day of the bright fortnight is <term>Kinstughna</term>; in the second half is <term>Bava</term>. In the second half of the fourteenth day of the dark fortnight, <term>Shakuni</term> is remembered. ॥13॥
<term>Chatushpada</term> is in the first half <insert>of the 15th/New Moon</insert>, and <term>Naga</term> in the second half. Thus, the knowledge related to the <term>Karanas</term> is always obtained. ॥14॥

**Meaning:** In the first half of the first day (<term>Pratipada</term>) of the bright fortnight, the Karana is Kinstughna, and in the second half, it is Bava. Similarly, in the second half of the fourteenth day of the dark fortnight, it is Shakuni and...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for calculating astrological components, specifically focusing on the Karana (half-day lunar cycles) and the Ishta-kaala (the elapsed time since sunrise at the moment of birth).</meta>

<page-num>5</page-num>
<header>The Sun of Birth-Charts Almanac</header>

In the first half of the New Moon <note>Amavasya</note>, the *Chatushpada* <term>Chatushpada: "Four-legged," the 10th fixed Karana</term> occurs, and in the second half, the *Naga* <term>Naga: "Serpent," the 11th fixed Karana</term> always occurs. These are called the fixed *Karanas*. ||13||14||

### On the Calculation of Karanas

->||Verse|| Count the birth date starting from the first day of the bright fortnight and double it.<-
->Subtract one and divide by seven; the remainder determines the Karana starting from Bava. ||15||<-

**Meaning:** Count from the first day of the bright lunar fortnight <note>Shukla Paksha</note> to the date of birth. Double that number, subtract one, and divide the result by seven. Count the remainder starting from the *Bava* Karana to find the birth Karana. ||15||

**Example:** If the birth is on the 8th day of the bright fortnight, the count is 8. Doubling it gives 16. Subtracting one leaves 15. Dividing by 7 leaves a remainder of 1. Therefore, the birth Karana is *Bava*. ||15||

### Knowledge of the Desired Time (Ishta-kaala)

->||Verse|| From the position of the Sun to the position of the Ascendant,<-
->Take five Ghatis for each sign, adjusting slightly as needed. ||16||<-

**Meaning:** Collect five *Ghatis* <term>Ghati: A traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> for every zodiac sign from the Sun's position to the Ascendant <note>Lagna</note>. Observe the degrees of the Sun and the Ascendant to slightly reduce or adjust the total. See the "Method" section for the specific details on adjusting these degrees. ||16||

**Method:** If the Sun's degrees are more than 15, take more *Ghatis* accordingly. If they are exactly 15, take 2.5 *Ghatis*. Similarly, if the degrees are 7.5, take 1.25 *Ghatis*. If the degrees are very low, take only a few *Palas* <term>Pala: 1/60th of a Ghati, or 24 seconds</term>. Apply the same logic to the Ascendant: if it is near 26 degrees, take nearly 5 *Ghatis*; if at 15 degrees, take 2.5; if at 7.5, take 1.25. For all other full signs in between, take 5 *Ghatis* each and sum them to find the *Ishta* <note>The time elapsed since sunrise</note>. ||16||

**Example:** In the birth chart of the boy Krishna, the Libra Ascendant has passed 26 degrees, so 3.5 *Ghatis* are taken for the Ascendant. The Sun is in Aquarius at 16 degrees (near 15), so 2.5 *Ghatis* are taken. The distance from the Sun to the Ascendant is 7 houses. Multiplying these by 5 gives 35. Adding the Sun's 2.5 and the Ascendant's 3.5, the *Ishta* is calculated as 41.25.

### Method for Positive and Negative Adjustments

->||Verse|| If the next row in the table is greater than one's own calculated time,<-
->The desired time must be subtracted; this movement is called 'Drig-abhidham'. ||17||<-

# Page 6
## Written by Astrologer Pandit Ayodhya Prasad Vaidyabhushan of Jhansi

**Meaning:** If the row in the almanac is ahead of your desired day, then subtract the desired time and day from the almanac's day and time (ghati). This results in a "negative correction" <note>original: "Rina Chalan"; a mathematical subtraction used to align the almanac to the local time</note>. ||17||

|| Verse || When the row is behind, subtract the row from your desired time. That correction is "positive," and the planet thus moved becomes clear. ||18||

**||Meaning||** If the row in the almanac is behind your desired day, then subtract it from the desired time—that is, decrease it—and this results in a "positive correction" <note>original: "Dhana Chalan"; despite the word "decrease," this refers to the additive adjustment process in the calculation</note>. ||18||

->|| Calculating the Planet's Position ||<-

|| Verse || Multiply the daily motion of the planet by the elapsed or remaining days and divide by sixty. With the resulting degrees, add or subtract to find the true planet at that moment. ||19||

**||Meaning||** Using the previously calculated positive or negative corrections, take the position and speed of all planets (except the Moon) as listed in the almanac. Multiply these using the <term>Gomutrika method</term> <note>A traditional "zigzag" or "cow's path" method of multiplication used in Indian mathematics for sexagesimal (base-60) calculations</note> and divide the result by sixty (60). The resulting degrees and minutes should be added to or subtracted from that same planet in the almanac. Specifically, if it was a positive correction, add them; if it was a negative correction, subtract them. ||19||

->|| Special Procedures for Planets ||<-

|| Verse || When a planet is moving forward, the wise should act as stated before. In retrogression, it is the opposite; for Rahu and Ketu, it is always otherwise. ||20||

**||Meaning||** If a planet is <term>Margi</term> <note>Direct motion; moving forward through the zodiac</note>, the wise person should add or subtract as previously described. However, if a planet is <term>Vakri</term> <note>Retrograde; appearing to move backward</note>, one must do the opposite: subtract where you would have added, and add where you would have subtracted. Note that for Rahu and Ketu <note>the lunar nodes</note>, the addition and subtraction are always performed in reverse. ||20||

->|| Method for Bhayat and Bhabhoga ||<-

|| Verse || Subtract the elapsed time of the constellation from ninety, or add the desired time from sunrise. These desired pulses of time are called <term>Bhayat</term>; when added to the current constellation's duration, it becomes <term>Bhabhoga</term>. ||21||

<note>The terms Bhayat and Bhabhoga refer to specific astronomical periods used to calculate the moon's position within a Nakshatra (lunar mansion) at the time of birth.</note>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for calculating planetary positions and lunar dates, following the introductory principles of the Janmanga Bhaskara Almanac.</meta>

<page-num>7</page-num>
->**The Sun of Birth-Charts Almanac**<-

**Meaning:** Subtract the elapsed time of the previous <term>Nakshatra</term><note>Lunar mansion or star cluster</note> (in <term>Ghati</term><note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> and other units) from 60. Place this value in two separate locations. In the first location, add the current <term>Ishta</term><note>The specific time of birth or event being calculated</note> time; the result is the <term>Bhayaat</term><note>The time elapsed within the current lunar mansion</note>. In the second location, add the time of the current Nakshatra; the result is the <term>Abhoga</term><note>The total duration of the lunar mansion</note>. ||21||

### Method for Calculating the Yearly Moonrise and True Moon Position:

**Verse:** Multiply the elapsed <term>Ghati</term> by sixty and divide by the <term>Abhoga</term>. Add this to the number of passed constellations multiplied by sixty and double the result. Divide by nine to find the degrees and further units. This is the method for finding the Moon's position as taught by the experts. ||22||

**Meaning:** Multiply the <term>Bhayaat</term> by 60, then divide it by the <term>Ghati</term> units of the <term>Abhoga</term>. The resulting quotient (in units of time) should be added to the count of constellations (starting from <term>Ashwini</term><note>The first of the 27 lunar mansions</note>) which has also been multiplied by 60. Double this entire sum (multiply by 2). Divide that total by 9; the quotient represents the Degrees. Multiply the remainder by 60 and divide by 9 again to find the <term>Kala</term><note>Minutes of arc</note>. Multiply that remainder by 60 and divide by 9 to find the <term>Vikala</term><note>Seconds of arc</note>. Divide the total degrees by 30 to determine the <term>Rashi</term><note>Zodiac Sign; one sign equals 30 degrees</note>. 

(Now, the method for calculating the daily motion): Multiply 48,000 by 60 and divide by the <term>Abhoga</term>. The quotient is the Moon's <term>Gati</term><note>Daily angular velocity or speed</note>. Multiply the remainder by 60 and divide by the <term>Abhoga</term> to find the <term>Vigati</term><note>Sub-units of daily motion</note>. ||22||

### Technique for Knowing the Daily Duration of Lunar Dates in Every Month of the Year

### ->According to the "Graha Laghava"<-
<note>The "Graha Laghava" is a famous 16th-century Sanskrit astronomical treatise by Ganesh Daivajna used to simplify planetary calculations.</note>

**Example:** In the year 1662 <note>Vikram Samvat, approx. 1605 CE</note>, on the 8th day of the bright fortnight of the month of <term>Phalguna</term>, the True Moon at morning was 1 sign, 16 degrees, 15 minutes, 12 seconds (1|16|15|12) with a speed of 778|33. The True Sun at morning was 10 signs, 13 degrees, 3 minutes, 14 seconds (10|13|3|14) with a speed of 60|15. Subtracting the Sun from the Moon leaves a remainder of 2 signs, 21 degrees, 12 minutes, 38 seconds (2|21|12|38) and a relative speed of 718|18. Converting this to degrees (81|12|38) and dividing by 12 <note>since a Tithi or lunar day is 12 degrees of separation</note>, the quotient is 7, which is the number of completed <term>Tithis</term>. The remaining degrees etc. are 9|12|38; this is the elapsed portion of the 8th <term>Tithi</term>. Subtracting this from 12 degrees leaves 2|47|22, which is the <term>Bhogya</term><note>remaining portion</note> of the lunar date. Converting this to <term>Vikala</term><note>seconds of arc</note> gives 32,062. Multiply this by 60 to get 1,923,720, then divide by the remaining speed of 718|18...

<meta>This page continues the astronomical calculations for the year 1962 of the Vikrama era, specifically focusing on the lunar and stellar positions for a birth chart.</meta>

<page-num>8</page-num>
<header>Created by the Astronomer Pandit Ayodhya Prasad Vaidya Bhushan of Jhansi</header>

By dividing the arc-seconds <note>original: "vikala"</note> 43028, the result was 44 Ghatis and 38 Palas <note>Ghati and Pala are traditional Indian units of time; 1 Ghati = 24 minutes</note>. This means that on the 8th day of the bright fortnight of the month of Phalguna in the year 1962, the time is 44 Ghatis and 38 Palas. This method should be understood and applied everywhere.

---

### Now, the method for knowing the Ghati and Pala of the daily Nakshatra (Lunar Mansion)

**||Example||** On the morning of the 8th day of the bright fortnight of Phalguna, Samvat 1962, the calculated position of the Moon was 2 signs, 6 degrees, 5 minutes, and 22 seconds. Converting this to minutes <note>original: "kala"</note> gives 75,712. Dividing this by 800 <note>the total minutes in one Nakshatra</note>, the quotient is 3, representing the completed Nakshatras. The remainder is 375 minutes and 22 seconds, which is the elapsed portion of the 4th Nakshatra, Rohini. Multiplying its arc-seconds (22,522) by 60 and dividing the result (1,351,320) by the Moon's daily motion (778 minutes, 33 seconds converted to 46,713 arc-seconds), the result is 28 Ghatis and 54 Palas of elapsed time <note>term: Bhukta - the portion of the star's duration already passed</note>. To find the remaining time <note>term: Bhogya - the remaining duration</note>, subtract 375 minutes and 22 seconds from 800 minutes, leaving 424 minutes and 48 seconds. Multiplying these arc-seconds (25,488) by 60 and dividing the result (1,529,280) by the Moon's motion (46,713 arc-seconds) gives 32 Ghatis and 44 Palas. Thus, on the 8th of the bright fortnight of Phalguna, Samvat 1962, the Rohini Nakshatra remains for 32 Ghatis and 44 Palas. Adding the elapsed and remaining portions gives a total duration of 61 Ghatis and 38 Palas.

->Obeisance to Lord Hari. Om.<-

According to the above method, this is the birth chart of the child Balakrishna: In the year 1962 of the Vikrama era (Saka era 1827), in the year named 'Pingala' <note>according to the 60-year Jovian cycle</note>, during the winter season, in the month of Phalguna, bright fortnight, 8th day, Saturday. At 44 Ghatis and 38 Palas, in the Rohini Nakshatra (remaining for 32 Ghatis and 44 Palas), at the time of birth the Moon enters Mrigashira. The Vishkumbha Yoga is 35 Ghatis and 51 Palas, and the current Karana is Vava. The day length is 28 Ghatis and 46 Palas, and the night is 31 Ghatis and 11 Palas. The Sun has passed 12 degrees of Aquarius <note>original: "Kumbha"</note> with 10 degrees remaining. The desired birth time <note>Ishta-kala</note> is 42 Ghatis and 15 Palas. The rising sign at birth <note>Lagna</note> is Libra <note>original: "Tula"</note> at 6 degrees and 26 minutes. According to this, the date is March 3, 1906 AD.

->Calculated Sun: 10 signs, 16 degrees, 50 minutes, 57 seconds. Motion: 60 minutes, 14 seconds.<-
->Calculated Moon: 2 signs, 24 degrees, 53 minutes, 6 seconds. Motion: 766 minutes, 22 seconds.<-

# The Sun of the Birth-Chart Almanac
<note>original: "Janmanga Bhaskara Panchanga"</note>

## || The Ascendant at the Birth of Lord Krishna ||
<note>The "Ascendant" or "Lagna" is the specific zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth, considered the most important point in a horoscope.</note>

Here begins the first chapter on the method of reconstructing lost birth charts, based on the "Sun of the Birth-Chart Almanac." || 1 ||

## Now, the second method for creating a birth chart based on the positions of the planets

-> That is to say <-

The process for creating a birth chart based solely on the positions of four planets in the horoscope—without needing the twelve zodiac signs or the ascendant—is as follows:

-> And an Example <-

For anyone born between the years 1700 and 1880 <note>Samvat era; approximately 1643 to 1823 CE</note>, they should look at their horoscope and identify which houses Saturn, Jupiter, Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon</note>, and the Sun are located in. Place these four planets in those exact positions and write the names of the zodiac signs above them, just as shown in the example of Lord Krishna’s horoscope.

-> Zodiac Names for Child Krishna <-

<term>Janmanga</term> <note>Body of the birth/horoscope</note>, <term>Bhaskara</term> <note>The Sun</note>, <term>Panchanga</term> <note>Almanac/Calendar</note>, <term>Janma Lagnam</term> <note>Birth Ascendant</note>, <term>Rashi</term> <note>Zodiac Sign</note>, <term>Lagna</term> <note>Rising Sign</note>, <term>Kundali</term> <note>Horoscope Chart</note>, <term>Graha</term> <note>Planet</note>, <term>Shani</term> <note>Saturn</note>, <term>Vrihaspati</term> <note>Jupiter</note>, <term>Rahu</term> <note>North Node</note>, <term>Surya</term> <note>Sun</note>, <term>Samvat</term> <note>The Vikram Samvat era calendar</note>

<summary>This introductory page of the Janmanga Bhaskara explains a specialized astrological technique for reconstructing a person's birth chart using only four key planets, using the horoscope of the deity Krishna as a primary example.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, birth chart, Lord Krishna, planetary positions, horoscopy, Samvat era, Indian almanac</keywords>

<header>Composed by the Astrologer Pandit Ayodhya Prasad Vaidya Bhushan Anjali</header>

### Now the Method for Determining the Year and Month is Written

In this horoscope <note>kundali</note>, the Sun and Saturn are in the fifth house, Jupiter is in the eighth house, and Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note> is in the tenth house. First, one must observe the distance in houses between Saturn and Jupiter, and the distance between Saturn and Rahu.

In Bal Krishna’s horoscope, Jupiter is fourth from Saturn, and Rahu is sixth from Saturn. Now, refer to the almanacs <note>panchangs</note> from the year 1800 to 1880 <note>Vikram Samvat era</note> which are printed in the Almanac Section of this very book. These contain the positions of the nine planets including their degrees for the twelve months across those 81 years. Look for the instance where Jupiter is fourth from Saturn and Rahu is sixth, while also ensuring the Sun is positioned with Saturn. Keep in mind that such a combination of these four planets will occur only once in these 81 years. We began searching for these four planets starting from the year 1800; not finding them in other years, we found all four planets aligned in the month of Phalguna <note>February/March</note> in the year 1862. This is direct proof: the year and month in which these four planets match perfectly should be declared the birth year and month. Therefore, Bal Krishna’s birth is stated to be in the month of Phalguna, Samvat 1862.

### Determining the Month from the Sun’s Zodiac Sign

**Verse** When the thousand-rayed one <note>the Sun</note> enters the sign of Aries, it is the first month. He enjoys one zodiac sign every month; of this, there is no doubt. ||1||

**Meaning** Always know the month of Vaishakha <note>April/May</note> by the Sun being in Aries. Following this sequence, calculate one month for each zodiac sign. ||1||

**Verse** From the signs of Aries to Pisces, and from the months of Vaishakha to Chaitra; thus should the wise man declare the month based on the Sun’s sign. ||2||

**Meaning** Understand the Sun’s sign and declare the month accordingly, from Aries to Pisces and from Vaishakha to Chaitra <note>the final month of the Hindu lunar calendar</note>. ||2||

**Example** For instance, Bal Krishna’s birth is in Phalguna. From Vaishakha to Phalguna is 11 months. Counting 11 signs from the Sun in Aries brings us to the Sun in Aquarius. Thus, the Sun with Saturn is in the 11th sign, which is Aquarius. Following this sequence, Jupiter is fourth from Aquarius, placing it in Taurus. Rahu is sixth from Saturn, placing it in Cancer in the house of action <note>the 10th house</note>. From this, the ascendant <note>Lagna</note> is declared to be Libra. ||2||

<note>original terms: Samvat (era), Rashi (zodiac sign), Kundali (horoscope), Panchang (almanac), Surya (Sun), Shani (Saturn), Brihaspati (Jupiter), Rahu (North Node), Mesha (Aries), Phalguna (11th month), Vaishakha (1st month), Kumbha (Aquarius), Tula (Libra), Jyotish (Astrology)</note>

## Knowledge of Ascendant Purification

> **Verse:** The wise should declare the Ascendant <term>Lagna; the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</term> purified if it falls within the trine <note>original: "Trikona"; the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</note> from the Moon. Alternatively, it is purified if the Moon is within the 7th or the trine from the Ascendant. ||3||

**Meaning:** If the Ascendant is within the trine—meaning the 9th or 5th positions—from the Moon, and if the Moon is within the 9th, 5th, or 7th houses from the Ascendant, then the Ascendant is said to be "purified" <note>meaning mathematically verified or auspicious</note>. ||3||

**Example:** In Balakrishna's birth chart, the Ascendant is 6th from the Moon, which is within the 9th house trine; therefore, consider it purified. ||3||

## Knowledge of the Date from the Month's Constellation

> **Verse:** The name of the month is famously derived from the constellation <term>Nakshatra; lunar mansion</term> associated with the Full Moon. From that, the wise scholar should calculate the constellation until the desired day. ||4||

**Meaning:** The constellations associated with the Full Moon are famous as the names of the months. From that starting point, a wise astrologer should calculate the count of constellations to determine the day of birth. ||4||

**The Monthly Constellations:** In Chaitra it is Chitra; in Vaishakh, Vishakha; in Jyeshtha, Jyeshtha; in Ashadh, Purvashadh; in Shravan, Shravan; in Bhadon, Purva Bhadrapad; in Ashwin, Ashwini; in Kartik, Krittika; in Margashirsha, Mrigasira or Ardra; in Paush, Pushya; in Magh, Magha; and in Phalgun, Uttara Phalguni. These constellations occur on the Full Moon of each respective month.

**Example:** For instance, if one wants to know the lunar date <term>Tithi</term> for the Mrigasira constellation in the month of Phalgun, Samvat 1962 <note>approx. 1905 CE</note>: The monthly constellation for the preceding month, Magh, is Magha. Counting from Magha to Mrigasira, there are 23 constellations. Since this is more than 15, divide by 15; the remainder is 8. Therefore, declare the birth to be on the 8th day of the bright fortnight <note>Shukla Paksha</note>. Alternatively, count backward from the Phalgun month constellation (Uttara Phalguni) to the birth constellation, subtract from 30, divide by 15, and the remainder is the date.

## Knowledge of Jupiter's Sign from the Era Year

<page-num>12</page-num>
<header>Written by the Astronomer-Physician Pandit Ayodhya Prasad Vaidyabhushan of Jhansi</header>

->|| Verse || From the birth year in the Shaka era, always subtract eighteen hundred (1800).<-
->Add seven (7) and divide by twelve (12); the remainder tells the position of Jupiter. ||5||<-

|| **Meaning** || Subtract 1800 from the Samvat year <note>The Vikram Samvat is a historical Hindu calendar era; to find the year in the Western calendar, one typically subtracts 57 years</note>. Then add 7 to that result. Divide the sum by 12. The remainder indicates the position of Jupiter—add one to the remainder to determine the fixed zodiac sign <term>Rashi</term> <note>The twelve signs of the zodiac, such as Aries or Taurus</note>. ||5||

|| **Example** || For instance, Balkrishna’s birth year is Samvat 1962. Subtracting 1800 leaves 162. Adding 7 makes it 169. Dividing by 12 leaves a remainder of 1. Adding 1 to that remainder equals 2; therefore, Jupiter is in Taurus <note>The second sign of the zodiac</note>. ||5||

### Determining the Year from Rahu

->|| Verse || Rahu remains in a sign for one and a half (1.5) years, moving in retrograde.<-
->By counting backwards from that, the knowledge of the year is certainly attained. ||6||<-

|| **Meaning** || Similarly, count backwards from the position of Rahu in the birth chart <term>Janmanga Kundli</term> to the position of Rahu in the current year. Multiply that count by 1.5. Subtract those elapsed years from the current Samvat year to find the birth year.

|| **Example** || For example, in Balkrishna’s chart, Rahu is in the 4th sign (Cancer), and in Samvat 1972, it is in the 10th sign (Capricorn). Counting backwards from Cancer to Capricorn gives 7 signs. Multiplying by 1.5 equals 10.5. To find the birth year, subtract 10 from 1972, which gives the birth year as 1962. However, for those older than 18 years, one must observe Saturn and Rahu to determine how many times Rahu has returned to its original sign. Multiply that cycle by 18, subtract it from the previously calculated year, and then declare the birth year.

**Notice:** Keep in mind that Saturn <term>Shani</term> occupies one zodiac sign for two and a half (2.5) years and returns to that same sign after 30 years. Similarly, Jupiter <term>Brihaspati</term> occupies a sign for thirteen months and returns to the same sign after twelve years. Rahu moves in retrograde <note>Appearing to move backwards through the zodiac</note>, occupying a sign for one and a half (1.5) years and returning to the same sign after 18 years.

<note>original vocabulary: Jyotirvid (Astronomer/Astrologer), Samvat (Era), Shloka (Verse), Rahu (North Lunar Node), Brihaspati (Jupiter), Shani (Saturn), Janmanga (Birth chart), Rashi (Zodiac sign)</note>

Note—Now that the Era <note>Samvat</note>, month, Ascendant <note>Lagna</note>, date, and the nine planets are known, the remaining details—such as the Shaka era, the sun's northern course <note>Uttarayana</note>, the day-half <note>Bava</note>, the lunar mansion <note>Nakshatra</note>, the union <note>Yoga</note>, and the action <note>Karana</note>—can be clearly determined. To find these, one must use the verses printed at the beginning of this book (numbered 3 to 16) according to the prescribed method and sequence.

### The Birth Chart According to the Second Method

In the auspicious Era <note>Samvat</note> 1962, Shaka 1827, in the month of Phalguna <note>February/March</note>, during the bright fortnight on the 8th day, a Saturday [at the time of] 48:38. The lunar mansion was Rohini at 32:54; at the time of birth, it moved from Mrigashira into the Vishkumbha union at 35:51. At that moment, the Karana was Chava. The day length was 28:46 and the night was 31:11. There, the degrees passed in Aquarius <note>Kumbha-Arka</note> were 16, and the degrees remaining were 10. The desired time <note>Ishtam</note> was 41:25. The body <note>Tanu</note> is 6:26; the birth occurred during the rising of the Libra Ascendant <note>Tula Lagna</note>.

> Verse: In the 15th year, the Moon situated in the body <note>the first house</note> causes pain from coughs; in the 27th year, it causes disease. In the 5th year, Mars <note>the son of the Earth</note> brings misfortune. In the 5th year, Saturn, Rahu, and Ketu <note>the shadow planets</note> bring sorrow. Mercury brings radiance in the 10th year.

If Mercury is present, radiance increases in the 20th year. If Jupiter is in the position of the body, then in the 8th year, one possesses beautiful and diverse intellect. If Venus is in the first house, then in the 17th year, the individual may approach another person's wife. ||1||

The Sun in the house of wealth causes loss of money in the 17th year. Acquisition of wealth occurs in the 27th year if the Moon is in the house of wealth; illness or expense in the 26th year if Mercury is there. Profit occurs in the 30th year if Jupiter is present, and great profit in the 6th year if Venus is in the second house. ||2||

**|| Meaning ||** If Ketu, Mars, Rahu, or Saturn are in the <term>House of Wealth</term><note>original: "Dhan Sthan" - the second house of the zodiac, governing finances and speech.</note>, wealth is destroyed in the 12th year. If the Sun is in the house of wealth, there is financial loss in the 17th year. If the Moon is in the house of wealth, wealth is acquired in the 27th year. If Mercury is there, there are expenses in the 26th year. If Jupiter is there, there is profit in the 30th year. If Venus is in the second house, there is profit in the 6th year. ||2||

**|| Verse ||** The Sun in the house of siblings brings wealth in the 20th year; the Moon brings profit in the 5th or 3rd year. Mars, Rahu, Ketu, or Saturn cause loss in the 13th year. Happiness from brothers comes in the 12th year from the Sun; Jupiter in the 27th or 20th year destroys the happiness of one's children. Jupiter gives desired happiness in the 10th year, and Venus brings pilgrimage to holy sites. ||3||

<page-num>18</page-num>
<header>Written by the Astrologer Pandit Ayodhya Prasad Vaidya Bhushan of Jhansi ( 18 )</header>

If <term>Jupiter</term><note>Sanskrit: Brihaspati</note> is in the ninth house, the mother may pass away in the 26th year. If Jupiter is in the ninth house, the father may be lost in the 15th year; however, if <term>Venus</term><note>Sanskrit: Shukra</note> is there, there will be gains in the 15th year. If <term>Saturn, Rahu, or Ketu</term><note>The latter two are the north and south lunar nodes, considered "shadow planets" in Vedic astrology</note> are in the ninth house, the father may be lost in the 23rd or 13th year. ॥6॥

---

->**॥Verse॥**<-
<note>The original verse is in Sanskrit, transcribed here into English meaning.</note>
If the Sun is in the house of action <note>The 10th house, known as Karma-sthana</note>, it causes separation in the 16th year. The Moon brings wealth in the 43rd or 27th year. Mars brings fear of weapons in the 54th year. Mercury brings wealth in its year. Jupiter brings wealth in the 16th, 12th, or 9th year. Venus brings financial happiness in the 4th or 15th year. Saturn and the others act like Mars. ॥10॥

**॥Meaning॥** If the <term>Sun</term><note>Sanskrit: Surya</note> is in the <term>tenth house</term><note>The house of profession and public life</note>, it causes separation in the 16th year. If the Moon is there, there is financial gain in the 43rd or 27th year. If <term>Mars</term><note>Sanskrit: Mangal</note> is there, there is fear from weapons and enemies in the 54th year. If <term>Mercury</term><note>Sanskrit: Budh</note> is in the tenth house, there is financial gain in the 17th year. If Jupiter is there, there is financial gain in the 9th, 12th, or 16th year. If Venus is there, one receives financial happiness in the 4th or 15th year. The results for Saturn, Rahu, and Ketu should be understood as being similar to Mars. ॥10॥

---

->**॥Verse॥**<-
If the Sun is in the house of gains <note>The 11th house, known as Labha-sthana</note>, the birth of a son occurs in the 20th or 24th year. The Moon brings the joy of gardens in the 20th, 45th, or 24th year. Ketu, Mars, and Saturn bring incomparable wealth and happiness. Mercury brings wealth in the 45th year. Jupiter and Venus bring the acquisition of wealth in the 12th or 16th year. ॥11॥

**॥Meaning॥** If the Sun is in the <term>eleventh house</term><note>The house of income, gains, and elder siblings</note>, the birth of a son occurs in the 20th or 24th year. If the Moon is there, then in the 20th, 45th, or 24th year, one acquires a garden or orchard...

...and there shall be happiness from property and the like. If Ketu, Rahu, Mars, or Saturn are positioned in the House of Gain <note>the 11th house</note>, then in the 45th year, there will be incomparable happiness from wealth and sons. If Mercury is there, then in the 12th or 16th year, financial happiness is obtained. If Jupiter or Venus are present, there will be gain of wealth in the 4th year. || 12 ||

|| Verse || Jupiter in the fifth year causes loss, while Venus in the 5th year <insert>5</insert> bestows financial happiness. The Sun causes loss of wealth in the 38th year <insert>38</insert>, and the Moon causes loss and suffering in the 3rd year. If Mercury, Mars, or Saturn are in the House of Expenditure <note>the 12th house</note> <insert>45</insert>, it causes bodily pain. Having gathered from the Horoscopy scriptures, I have collected here this essence of Astrology. || 12 ||

|| Meaning || If Jupiter is in the House of Expenditure <note>original: "Vyaya Sthana"</note>, there is loss in the 5th year. If Venus is there, one receives financial happiness in the 5th year. If the Sun is in the 12th house, there is financial loss in the 38th year. If the Moon is there, there is loss and suffering in the 3rd year. If Mercury, Mars, Saturn, Rahu, or Ketu are in the House of Expenditure, there will be suffering for the wife in the 45th year. This book has been composed according to the methods of the Astrology scriptures by Pandit Ayodhya Prasad Mishra, a resident of Jhansi and a decorated Physician-Astrologer <term>Vaidya Bhushan: a title meaning 'Jewel of Physicians'</term>. || 12 ||

->( From the Great Essence of Astrology, the auspicious and inauspicious combinations are told )<-

|| Verse || When Venus, the Priest of the Gods <note>Jupiter</note>, and the Moon are situated in the Cardinal Houses <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term>, men achieve great happiness, increase in wealth, and success in their works. When the Son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note> and the Moon are in the Ascendant, and the Sun and Jupiter are in the Trines, and the Son of the Earth <note>Mars</note> is in the tenth house, they call this a Royal Combination <term>Raja Yoga: a planetary alignment indicating high status or power</term>. || 1 ||

|| Meaning || It is said that one should recognize a Royal Combination when Venus, Jupiter, and the Moon are in the Cardinal houses, Saturn and the Moon are in the Ascendant <note>the 1st house</note>, and the Sun and Jupiter are in the Trines <note>the 5th or 9th houses</note>. || 1 ||

<page-num>20</page-num>
<header>Written by the Astronomer-Astrologer Pandit Ayodhya Prasad Vaidya Bhushan of Jhansi</header>

## || Knowledge of the Outcaste Combination from the Brihat Jyotish Patha ||

|| Verse || If Jupiter is in the house of wealth <note>the 2nd house</note>, Mars is in the house of siblings <note>the 3rd house</note>, Venus is in the seventh house, and Saturn and the Moon are in the eighth house: even if a son is born to a Brahmin <note>the highest priestly caste</note>, he becomes an outcaste <term>Mleccha: originally meaning a non-Vedic foreigner, here implying one who falls from social/religious grace</term>; of this there is no doubt. || 2 ||

|| Meaning || One who has Jupiter in the house of wealth, Mars in the natural <note>sibling</note> house, Venus in the seventh house, and Saturn and the Moon in the eighth house—if such a combination exists, then even if the child is the son of a Brahmin, he becomes an outcaste. || 2 ||

## || Knowledge of the Death Combination from the Brihat Jyotish Patha ||

|| Verse || If cruel planets <note>malefics like Mars or Saturn</note> are in the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the rising sign</term> and the Moon is in the sixth or eighth house, then the death of the newborn will occur immediately; of this there is no doubt. || 3 ||

|| Meaning || If there are cruel planets in the Ascendant and the Moon is in the sixth or eighth house, then one should know that the death of that child will occur quickly. || 3 ||

## || Knowledge of the Lifespan Combination from the Brihat Jyotish Patha ||

|| Verse || When Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note> is in the fourth house and the Moon is in a cardinal house <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term>, death will occur in the twentieth year; of this there is no doubt. || 4 ||

|| Meaning || He who has Rahu in the fourth house and the Moon in a cardinal house, his lifespan should be known as 20 years. || 4 ||

|| Verse || When Rahu is in the seventh house at the time of birth, then death will occur in the tenth year; of this there is no doubt. || 5 ||

## The Sun of Birth-Charts Almanac

|| Meaning || If Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon, often depicted as a shadow planet or dragon's head</note> is seated in the seventh house of the birth ascendant, one should know the lifespan to be ten years. || 5 ||

|| Verse || When the Moon is in the twelfth house, or in the sixth or eighth house joined with malefic planets, the child becomes short-lived, even if Lord Shiva <note>original: "Shankara"</note> himself protects them. || 6 ||

|| Meaning || If at the time of birth the Moon is in the twelfth house, or if the Moon is in the sixth or eighth house, one should know that child to be short-lived <term>Alpayu (short life)</term> even if Lord Shiva protects them. || 6 ||

|| Verse || When the Sun is in the sign of Saturn, or Saturn is in the sign of the Sun, death occurs in the twentieth year, even if Indra <note>The King of the Gods</note> himself protects them. || 7 ||

|| Meaning || If the Sun is in Saturn’s domain <note>Capricorn or Aquarius</note> or Saturn is in the Sun’s domain <note>Leo</note>, one with such a planetary combination will not live beyond twenty years, even if Indra protects them. || 7 ||

|| Verse || When Saturn, Rahu <note>original: "Saihikeya," a name for Rahu meaning 'son of Simhika'</note>, and Mars <note>original: "Dharatmaja," meaning 'son of the Earth'</note> are in the house of wealth <note>the 2nd house</note>, and Venus and Jupiter are in the seventh, and the Sun and Moon are in the eighth; or if in the house of Mercury <note>original: "Brahma-putra," son of Brahma</note>, he always delights in the daughters of harlots; upon reaching the twentieth year, he becomes an outcast <term>Mleccha (foreigner or non-believer)</term>, without a doubt. || 8 ||

|| Meaning || He who has Saturn, Rahu, and Mars in the house of wealth, and Jupiter and Venus in the seventh house, and the Sun and Moon in the eighth house—if such a combination exists, know it as the "Loss of Caste" combination <note>original: "Jati Ansh Yoga"</note> and know the lifespan to be 20 years. || 8 ||

|| Verse || When the Sun is in the sixth or eighth house at the time of birth, the death of the born child occurs through the "Fault of the King," of this there is no doubt. || 9 ||

|| Meaning || He who has the Sun seated in the sixth or eighth house, his death should be known to occur through the "Fault of the King" <note>likely referring to legal punishment, state execution, or political misfortune</note>. || 9 ||

# Composed by the Astrologer Pandit Ayodhya Prasad Vaidya Bhushan of Jhansi

-> || Brihat Jyotish Patha: Combinations for Death || <-
<note>original: "Vri. Jyo. Pa. Mrityu Yogah". This refers to a section on 'Mrityu Yoga'—astrological alignments indicating death or severe misfortune.</note>

> || Verse || If the Sun, Rahu, Mars, or Saturn are situated in the Ascendant or the fifth house, they destroy the father, mother, brother, and oneself respectively. || 10 ||
<note>The Ascendant (Lagna) is the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth.</note>

|| Meaning || If the Sun, Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon</note>, Mars, and Saturn are placed in the Ascendant or the fifth house, then the results follow in this order: the Sun affects the father, Rahu affects the mother, Mars affects the brother, and Saturn should be known as inauspicious for oneself. || 10 ||

> || Verse || When Saturn is in the Ascendant, the Moon is in the sixth house, and Mars is in the seventh house, the father of that person does not live. || 11 ||

|| Meaning || For one who has Saturn in the sixth house <note>Note: The Hindi commentary here says sixth, while the Sanskrit verse says Ascendant; the commentary likely follows a specific local tradition</note> and the Moon and Mars in the seventh house, his father shall die. || 11 ||

> || Verse || When Mars is in the birth Ascendant and Jupiter is in the eighth house, death occurs in the twelfth year, even if protected by Lord Shiva. || 12 ||

|| Meaning || If Mars is in the birth Ascendant and Jupiter is in the eighth house, one should know that death may occur in the twelfth year. || 12 ||

> || Verse || Rahu in the tenth house causes suffering to the father and mother; death occurs for the newborn within twelve years. || 13 ||

|| Meaning || If Rahu is in the tenth house from the Ascendant, it brings sorrow to the parents and death is indicated up to the age of twelve. || 13 ||

-> ( Statement on the offspring in the womb, son or daughter ) <-

> || Verse || Combine the lunar date, the day of the week, the lunar mansion, and the letters of the woman's name; divide by seven. If the remainder is even, it is a girl; if odd, a son. || 14 ||

|| Meaning || When someone asks whether a particular woman will have a son or a daughter, take the <term>Nakshatra</term> <note>Lunar mansion/star cluster</note>, the <term>Tithi</term> <note>Lunar day</note>, the day of the week at that moment, and the letters of the woman's name. Total them and divide by seven. If the remainder is an even number, say it will be a girl; if the remainder is an odd number, say it will be a son. || 14 ||

-> This concludes the section on Predictive Astrology in the Shri Janmanga Bhaskara Almanac. || 3 || <-

# ( A Calendar for Four Thousand Three Hundred 4300 Years )

For example, if you want to find out what day of the week it was on July 28, 1515: locate the row for 1500 and move downwards, then move from left to right in the row for 15; where they intersect, you find Friday <note>original: "Shuk" for Shukravar</note>. From January 1 to July 28, there are 209 days. Dividing by 7, the remainder is 6. Counting 6 days forward from Friday, we arrive at Wednesday. Thus, the day you sought was Wednesday.

**Method —** To find the day of the week for any date in any month of any year, look for that number in the table below and move your finger to the right. Similarly, move your finger downwards from the century <term>shatabdi</term>. The point where both fingers meet is the base day; set this aside. Then, as shown in the example, add up the total elapsed days of that year and divide by 7. Count forward from the base day by the number of the remainder. That will be the desired day. If the remainder is zero, then the day immediately preceding the base day should be understood.

| | | | | | | | | | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **January** | ... | **31** | **July** | ... | **31** | 0 | 100 | 200 | 300 |
| **February** | ... | **28** | **August** | ... | **31** | 400 | 500 | 600 | 700 |
| **March ...** | ... | **31** | **September** | ... | **30** | 800 | 900 | 1000 | 1100 |
| **April** | ... | **30** | **October** | ... | **31** | 1200 | 1300 | 1400 | 1500 |
| **May** | ... | **31** | **November** | ... | **30** | 1600 | 1700 | 1800 | 1900 |
| **June ...** | ... | **30** | **December** | ... | **31** | 2000 | 2100 | 2200 | 2300 |
| | | | | | | 2400 | 2500 | 2600 | 2700 |
| | | | | | | 2800 | 2900 | 3000 | 3100 |
| | | | | | | 3200 | 3300 | 3400 | 3500 |
| | | | | | | 3600 | 3700 | 3800 | 3900 |
| | | | | | | 4000 | 4100 | 4200 | 4300 |

| 4 | 4 | 11 | 18 | 25 | 32 | 39 | 46 | 53 | 60 | 67 | 74 | 81 | 88 | 95 | 0 | 0 | Thu | Tue | Sun | Fri |
| 5 | 5 | 12 | 19 | 26 | 33 | 40 | 47 | 54 | 61 | 68 | 75 | 82 | 89 | 96 | 0 | 0 | Fri | Wed | Mon | Sat |

**Note —** Century years should be divided by 400 and ordinary years by 4. In any year where there is no remainder, February will have 29 days <note>This explains the rule for Leap Years</note>.

## In the auspicious year Samvat 1929, Shaka 1794, in the year named Shubha <note>Samvat and Shaka are traditional Indian eras; 1929 Samvat corresponds to approximately 1872 CE. 'Shubha' is the name of this specific year in the 60-year Jovian cycle.</note>

->(Ointment for Ringworm.) Box available at A.P. Depot, Jhansi<-
<note>This is a commercial advertisement for a medical ointment, common in 19th-century almanacs.</note>

<term>Almanac (Panchang)</term>: A traditional Hindu calendar that tracks five key astronomical elements.
<term>Lunar Day (Tithi)</term>: A duration in which the longitudinal angle between the Sun and Moon increases by 12 degrees.
<term>Planet (Graha)</term>: In this context, includes the Sun, Moon, and nodes (Rahu).
<term>Samvat/Shaka</term>: Eras used in the Indian dating system.
<term>Months (Vaisakha to Phalguna)</term>: The twelve months of the Hindu lunar calendar.
<term>Planetary Names</term>: Sun (Surya), Moon (Chandra), Mars (Mangal), Mercury (Budh), Jupiter (Guru), Venus (Shukra), Saturn (Shani), Rahu.

<summary>This page presents a comprehensive astronomical table for the Hindu year 1874-1875, detailing the positions of planets and lunar phases for each month. It serves as a historical record of timekeeping and includes a local advertisement for medicinal ointment from Jhansi.</summary>
<keywords>Hindu calendar, astronomy, Jhansi, 19th century, planetary positions, lunar months, Samvat 1931, Shaka 1796</keywords>

<note>Technical Vocabulary:
**Samvat/Shaka**: Eras of the Indian calendar.
**Tithi**: A lunar day.
**Graha**: Planets (including Sun, Moon, and nodes Rahu/Ketu).
**Nakshatra**: Lunar mansion or star cluster.
**Yoga/Karana**: Mathematical divisions of time used to determine auspiciousness.
**Planets**: Sun (Surya), Moon (Chandra), Mars (Mangal), Mercury (Budh), Jupiter (Brihaspati/Vri), Venus (Shukra), Saturn (Shani), Rahu (North Node).</note>

<header>By Pandit Ayodhya Prasad Vaidyabhushan, Astrologer of Jhansi</header>

# Now, the Consideration of Questions Regarding Actions and Non-Actions

> Verse: Add the direction <note>the direction the seeker is facing</note>, the watch of the day <note>Prahar: a three-hour period</note>, the lunar mansion <note>Nakshatra</note>, and the day of the week. Divide the total by eight; the remainder indicates the nature of the question.
> If one or five remain, success is swift; if six or four, success comes in three days; if three or seven, there is a delay in success; if two or eight, success will not be achieved.

**Meaning—** Note the direction the questioner is facing, the current watch of the day, the lunar mansion, and the day of the week. Combine these values and divide by 8. Based on the remainder, provide the following results: If 1 or 5 remain, the work will be accomplished quickly. If 6 or 4 remain, the work will be accomplished in 3 days. If 3 or 7 remain, the work will be accomplished after a delay. If 2 or 8 remain, the work will not be accomplished.

## Now, the Auspicious Timing for the Ritual Bath after Childbirth

> The two Punarvasus, Chitra, Vishakha, the two Bharanis, Mula, Magha, and Shravana (the tenth) should be avoided.
> Monday, Friday, Wednesday, and Saturday are for the birth-bath ritual; avoid the first, sixth, and ninth lunar days, as well as the diminished lunar day <note>Tithi-kshaya: a day where a lunar date is skipped in the calendar</note>.

-> These should be abandoned —<-

## The Auspicious Timing for the Bride’s First Entry into the Home

> In the three Hastas, the two Rohinis, Magha, Pushya, Dhanishta, Shravana, and the three Uttaras—
> Also Mula, Anuradha, Ashwini, and Revati; the bride’s entry should occur during fixed ascendants <note>Sthira Lagna: zodiac signs like Taurus or Leo that represent stability</note>.

## The Process for Verifying Marriage Compatibility

| | | | |
|----------|----------|----------|----------|
| Worshipful Jupiter in Marriage | 10 | 6 | 1 | 2 | Worshipful Sun | 1 | 2 | 5 | 7 | 9 |
| Pure Jupiter | 9, 1, 11, 2, 7 | Pure Sun | 3 | 6 | 10 : 11 |
| Pure Moon | 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 9, 10, 11 | Unfavorable Jupiter, Sun, Moon | 4 | 8 | 12 |

## Now, Knowledge of the Directional Spear <note>Dishashul: directions to avoid traveling in on specific days</note>

Moon and Saturn: East; Jupiter: South; Sun and Venus: West; Mercury and Mars: North.

## Knowledge of the Time-Rahu <note>The position of the shadow planet Rahu throughout the week</note>

Saturn: East; Jupiter: South; Mars: West; Sun: North; Venus: Southeast; Mercury: Southwest; Moon: Northwest.

## Knowledge of the Yogini <note>The position of the mystical female spirits/energies on specific lunar dates</note>

1st and 9th: East; 3rd and 11th: Southeast; 5th and 13th: South; 4th and 12th: Southwest; 6th and 14th: West; 7th and 15th: Northwest; 2nd and 10th: North; 8th and 16th: Northeast.

<meta>This page continues the astrological and calendrical instructions from the Janmadyabhaskara Almanac. The text transitions from planetary positions to practical methods for time-keeping and auspicious timing for daily activities.</meta>

# Page 93
## Janmadyabhaskara Almanac
<meta>The header refers to a specific type of traditional Indian calendar or almanac (Panchang) likely named after a scholar or a specific lineage of sun-based calculations.</meta>

## Knowledge of the Moon’s Residence

Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius are in the East; Taurus, Virgo, and Capricorn are in the South; Gemini, Libra, and Aquarius are in the West; Cancer, Pisces, and Scorpio are in the North. <note>This refers to the 'Chandra-vasa' or the direction the Moon is considered to reside in based on the zodiac sign it currently occupies, used to determine auspicious directions for travel.</note>

---

## Knowledge of Time

> A gnomon <term>Shanku: a vertical pillar or stick used in sundials to measure time by its shadow</term> should be made of three fingers' length and combined with the length of its shadow; divide sixty-four by this sum, and the remainder is known as the measures of time. <note>original Latin/Sanskrit: "Angulam shankukartavyam..."</note>

**Meaning**—Construct a gnomon of three fingers' height and place it on level ground in direct sunlight to measure the shadow. Add three to the length of the shadow measured. Divide 64 by this total. The resulting whole number gives the <term>Ghati: a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> and the remainder gives the <term>Pala: a unit equal to 24 seconds</term>. If calculated before noon, the result shows the time passed since sunrise; if after noon, it shows the time remaining until sunset.

> In the year 1772 <note>Vikram Samvat calendar, roughly 1715 CE</note>, on a Saturday during the bright fortnight of the month of Shravana, this work was composed for the pleasure of the virtuous.

->May it be auspicious.<-

---

## Auspicious Moments for Making Jewelry, as stated by Vashistha

**Verse**—In the swift, mobile, and soft constellations, excluding the "empty" lunar days and the new moon;
->On all days of the week, including the "Tripushkara" yoga, jewelry should be fashioned.<-

**Meaning**—One should have jewelry made during the constellations of Hasta, Ashwini, Pushya, Abhijit, Swati, Punarvasu, Shravana, Dhanistha, and Shatataraka. Avoid the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days <note>known as Rikta or 'empty' days</note> and the New Moon. Specifically, the 2nd, 12th, and 7th lunar days are good. Avoid Saturdays, Sundays, and Tuesdays, and the constellations of Vishakha, Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Bhadrapada, Krittika, and Uttarashada.

---

## Constellations for Buying and Selling, from the Deepika

**Verse**—The constellations starting with Bharani, Jyeshtha, Krittika, and Purva-Phalguni are undesirable for buying but excellent for selling.
->Revati, Ashwini, Chitra, Shatataraka, Shravana, and Swati are excellent for buying but forbidden for selling.<-

**Meaning**—Selling is auspicious in the constellations of Bharani, Jyeshtha, Krittika, and Purva-Phalguni, but buying in these is unfavorable. In Revati, Ashwini, Chitra, Shatataraka, Shravana, and Swati, buying is auspicious, but selling is unfavorable.

---

## Auspicious Moments for Taking Medicine, according to Vashistha

<summary>This page provides astrological guidelines for determining the Moon's direction, calculating time using a sundial gnomon, and selecting auspicious constellations for commerce and jewelry making.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, gnomon, sundial, jewelry making, commerce, lunar days, constellations, Vashistha, timekeeping</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the astrological and medical guidelines provided by Pandit Ayodhyaprasad, focusing on auspicious timing for health, weaponry, and infrastructure.</meta>

<page-num>94</page-num>
<header>Written by Pandit Ayodhyaprasad Vaidyabhushan, Astrologer of Jhansi—</header>

> Verse—In the three stars of Hasta <note>Hasta, Chitra, and Swati</note>, Pushya, Punarvasu, the three stars of Vishnu <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha</note>, Ashwini, and Revati;
> In Anuradha, Mrigashira, and Mula, on Sundays and other auspicious days, the consumption of medicine is declared beneficial. || 3 ||

Meaning—In the three constellations starting from Hasta, Punarvasu, Pushya, and the three constellations starting from Shravana, as well as Ashwini, Revati, Anuradha, Mrigashira, and Mula—on Sundays and other auspicious days—taking medicine is auspicious. || 3 ||

# Auspicious Moments for Bearing Swords and Enjoying Beds; As stated in the *Vyavaharocchaya* <note>A classical compendium on astrological practices</note>.

> Verse—In Pushya, Punarvasu, Chitra, the three Uttaras, Revati,
> Ashwini, Hasta, Vishakha, and Anuradha; on the days of the Sun, Jupiter, and Venus;
> When the Moon is in Aquarius, Scorpio, Taurus, or Leo and aspected by benefics,
> The donning of armor, arrows, swords, spears, and knives is beneficial for kings. || 4 ||

Meaning—In the constellations of Pushya, Punarvasu, Chitra, the three Uttaras <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Rohini, Hasta, Abhijit, Jyeshtha, Vishakha, Mrigashira, Revati, and Anuradha; avoiding the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days; on Fridays, Thursdays, and Sundays—it is auspicious to bear spears and other weapons. When the Moon and Sun are favorable in the signs of Aquarius, Cancer, Taurus, or Capricorn; and in the constellations of Anuradha, Mrigashira, Punarvasu, Bharani, Pushya, Shravana, Rohini, and the three Uttaras; on the days of the Moon <note>Monday</note>, Jupiter, Mercury, and Venus—it is auspicious to bear swords or sit in palanquins and similar carriages.

# Auspicious Moments for Digging a Well.

Starting from the constellation of Rohini, one should consider the "Well-Cycle" <term>Kupa-Chakra: A divinatory diagram used to find water</term> by placing the constellations in groups of three. In the center, it yields excellent water; in the East, it causes loss of land. In the Southeast, it causes "water-burning" <note>likely meaning the well will run dry or be bitter</note>; in the South, there is a delay in finding water. In the Southwest, the water is like nectar; in the West, it brings happiness and wealth. In the Northwest, it causes water-burning; in the North, there is a delay in finding water. In the Northeast, it brings happiness, prosperity, and is the giver of all auspiciousness.

---

| Northeast | East | Southeast |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Ashwini, Bharani, Krittika | Punarvasu, Pushya, Ashlesha | Magha, Purva Phalguni, Uttara Phalguni |
| **North** <br/> Purva Bhadrapada, Uttara Bhadrapada, Revati | **Center** <br/> Rohini, Mrigashira, Ardra | **South** <br/> Hasta, Chitra, Swati |
| **Northwest** <br/> Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha | **West** <br/> Mula, Purva Ashadha, Uttara Ashadha | **Southwest** <br/> Vishakha, Anuradha, Jyeshtha |

---

-> Seeking your grace, Vaidyabhushan <-

## Holy Year 1966 of the Vikram Era, 1831 of the Shaka Era <note>This corresponds to the years 1909–1910 CE. The Bhrigu Samhita is a vast collection of predictive astrology attributed to the Vedic sage Bhrigu.</note>

| Lunar Date (Tithi) | Planetary Positions (Graha) |

### ( Annual Table )

->( Method for calculating the year )<-

To determine the <term>Yearly Fruit</term><note>Varshaphala: The astrological predictions for a specific year of a person's life.</note>, subtract the year of birth from the current <term>Samvat</term><note>The Vikram Samvat is a historical Hindu calendar era; it is approximately 57 years ahead of the Gregorian calendar.</note> year. The remainder should be understood as the "elapsed years." Take the <term>Yearly Constant</term><note>Varshadhruva: A fixed mathematical value used in Hindu astronomy to calculate the exact moment the sun returns to its natal position.</note> for that number; add the birth weekday to the "Day" value and add the birth time (<term>Ishta</term>) to the <term>Ghati-Pala</term><note>Traditional units of time: 1 Ghati = 24 minutes; 1 Pala = 24 seconds.</note> values. By establishing the <term>Yearly Ascendant</term><note>Varsha Lagna: The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the exact moment of the solar return.</note> from this, one may declare the results.

(As an example) Bal Krishna was born in the year <note>Vikram Samvat</note> 1662. By subtracting this from the year 1672, the remainder is 10. In the table, the constant value <note>Dhruva</note> is 5 | 35 | 15 | 00. Bal Krishna's birth day is 7 (Saturday). The desired time <note>Ishtam</note> is 41 | 15. By adding the day to the day and the time to the time, the result is 6 | 16 | 30 | 00. Since the Sun is at 10 | 16, the solar return occurs in the year 1672 when the Sun is at 16 degrees of Aquarius, on Friday the 14th day of the dark fortnight of the month of Phalguna. Thus, the year commenced on that day. The time is 16 | 30 | 00. The Ascendant <note>Tanu</note> is 2 | 15 Gemini. The year has entered. 11 completed cycles, 10 remaining. Auspiciousness.

# The Essence of Law
